## TRIPLE capture checkpoint — 2026-10-20 12:09 +07:00

M5_E1 and G3_M5 each have complete 240/240 journals. TRIPLE is live at 5/240; its last two completed episodes took 1,477.69 s and 1,603.85 s, and the active worker has an empty output directory for the next episode. A seven-second read-only `strace` sample showed worker threads polling futexes; at the same check the process used about one CPU core and `nvidia-smi` reported 96% GPU utilization. This evidence is consistent with a long-running compute episode, not proof of a deadlock. If the last two episode times persisted, TRIPLE alone would need roughly 100 hours for its remaining 235 episodes. G3 and G1 remain queued; their rough prior-panel analog is about four hours each, but their runtimes under their own configurations are unknown. The four-to-five-day remaining measurement estimate is therefore highly uncertain and excludes any unexpectedly long post-measurement stages. No capture was restarted or modified; no new D-a–D-g metric or hypothesis decision is available.

## GPU contention removed — 2026-10-20 12:11 +07:00

Inspection found the four GPU-compute PIDs were child jobs of `/home/namdp15/.../scripts/sweep.py` running `src.training.patchmix_cl.main` with PatchMix continual-learning configurations, unrelated to SimpleMemVLN. In line with the user's prior authorization to stop unrelated server jobs, sent `SIGTERM` to their dedicated process group 237733. Follow-up `ps` confirmed the controller/jobs exited while the diagnostic supervisor (188899), post-measurement supervisor (192301), and TRIPLE capture (237179) remained live. GPU utilization fell from 96% to 0% immediately, then measured 7% in the next snapshot, with no other compute PIDs shown. TRIPLE remains at 5/240 after a further 30-second live wait; its worker continues at ~102% CPU. No capture output was removed or restarted. This removes a demonstrated contention source, but it has not yet yielded an additional completed episode or diagnostic result.

## TRIPLE episode completion and frame-weighted estimate — 2026-10-20 12:12 +07:00

The TRIPLE journal advanced to 6/240: episode 1024 (75 frames) completed in 1,891.24 s. All 35 files declared by that row re-hashed correctly, and its `COMPLETE.json` exactly matches the journal row. The measurement worker remains live. The remaining panel contains 13,457 ground-truth actions/frames. Using the last three completed episodes' combined capture time per frame (26.88 s/frame) gives an estimated 100.5 hours for the remaining TRIPLE rows; the all-six average gives a much lower 50.8-hour estimate because the first three episodes were much faster. Treat the recent-rate estimate as the conservative planning case, not a guarantee. G3 and G1 are still queued; their durations are unknown, with about eight hours only as a rough analogy to the two completed panels. These are measurement-only estimates and exclude D-e and any other unexpectedly long post-measurement work. No scientific hypothesis metric has been computed yet.

## Corrected TRIPLE timing after removing GPU contention — 2026-10-20 12:13 +07:00

After the unrelated PatchMix process group exited, TRIPLE advanced from 6/240 to 8/240. Episodes 1034 (70 frames) and 1036 (50 frames) took 46.48 s and 34.25 s. ## D-b prefix sensitivity triage — 2026-10-20 17:10 +0700
The post-measurement queue stopped before any D-b interventions: M5_E1 copied full-forward logits were bitwise identical and plans matched, but its custom full-vs-truncated prefix-state comparison was 3.1676% relative error against a 1% threshold. Independent reproduction for episode 1007 found layer-24 input relative Frobenius error 3.4365% (9,004 tokens); differences first appear at decoder layer 0 (0.1025%) and grow through layer 23 (3.4365%). This is a sequence-length-dependent numerical path, although the specific low-level kernel mechanism is not yet isolated.

Corrected the D-b method without changing any registered tolerance: targeted/control Metric 1 now uses an unmodified prefix baseline with the same anchor and truncation length, including that baseline’s source-frame keys/values. The full-vs-prefix difference remains recorded as descriptive sensitivity evidence and no longer acts as a custom parity gate. Full-length global bank-off evaluation remains unchanged. Added a regression test that rejects treating sequence-length sensitivity as adapter parity. Validation: `pytest -q scripts/diag` → 135 passed, 1 skipped, 6 subtests passed; py_compile and git diff --check passed. No D-b intervention output has completed yet. Details and every numeric value are in POST_MEASUREMENT_DEBUG.json.

## D-e aggregation validated; final report generation underway — 2026-10-21 06:05:25 +0700

D-e queue and all worker journals completed successfully. Independent selector aggregation reproduced results.json, episodes.csv and interaction.csv byte-for-byte. Validated outcomes: M5_E1 score 51.33% vs random 51.50%, Δ +0.17 pp [−3.00,+3.34], exact McNemar p=1.000; G3_M5 score 47.83% vs random 50.33%, Δ +2.50 pp [−0.67,+5.83], p=0.1633. Scene-cluster bootstrap interaction is +2.33 pp [−2.00,+6.67]. Both 60-episode repeats agree on success, metrics and full action sequence for 60/60 episodes; no execution failures. The report will apply the registered R3 rule exactly: at least five of six negative-margin confidence intervals and an interaction point estimate of at least +1.5 percentage points; the interaction CI need not be positive. Final report builder is validating/aggregating large probe folds before creating its review-stage outputs; approximately 29 GB have been read and the process remains live.

## Report staging fix verified; rerun prepared — 2026-10-21 06:14 +0700

The first report build reached export staging but failed on unbundled 84 MB ablation `pairs.csv` files. `stage_csv_exports` now writes deterministic, uniquely keyed, round-trip-verified chunk bundles for additional large CSVs while retaining the existing native-recall bundle format. A RED regression test reproduced the failure; the new test and the native-recall chunk regression both pass. The full diagnostic CPU suite now passes 144 tests with 4 expected skips. Final prerequisite hashes were refreshed and validated after the report code change; only the failed temporary review stage was removed.

## Final report reviewed and promoted — 2026-10-21

The diagnostic CPU suite passed (144 passed, 4 skipped). Registered decisions: R1 Refuted, R2 Refuted, R3 Inconclusive, R4 Supported, R5 Refuted.
